C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

头颈癌临床前模型中 HPV 靶向治疗 疫苗 的免疫学和抗肿瘤反应:系统评价。

Immunological and antitumor responses to HPV-targeted therapeutic vaccines in preclinical models of head and neck cancer: a systematic review.
作者： Sara Fraser, Darya Tamashekan, Marco Mascarella, Livia Florianova, Grégoire B. Morand, Alex M. Mlynarek, Michael P. Hier, Sabrina D. S. Wurzba
摘要中文翻译人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关的头颈鳞状细胞癌 (HNSCC) 越来越普遍,占 HNSCC 的 22-70%,具体取决于地区。尽管预防性 HPV 疫苗 在 2000 年代初被批准用于预防 HPV 感染和肿瘤发生,但它们对已确定的 HPV 感染和相关癌症无效。这些 HPV 相关口咽癌的护理标准结合了放疗、手术和化疗。然而,对于在过去十年中没有新的 治疗 进入常规临床实践的患者来说,这些 治疗 可能会导致显着的终生副作用。治疗性癌症 疫苗 为 HPV 相关癌症的新型 治疗 方法提供了机会,特别是因为这些癌症显示病毒肿瘤特异性抗原(TSA),如 E6 和 E7,使它们很容易被这种类型的免疫疗法靶向。目前,尚无 FDA 批准的针对 HPV 相关癌症的治疗药物 疫苗,但该领域的临床前和临床研究正在进行中。在这篇系统综述中,我们描述了当前的 HPV 疫苗接种 策略、其生物学机制及其在临床前癌症模型中的免疫学和治疗效果。我们讨论治疗 疫苗 的每个组成部分:抗原、递送平台、佐剂和组合药物。我们还评估了最近评估 HPV 治疗 疫苗 的临床前研究的结果,以对这种 治疗 模式的未来进行展望。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述将 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关头颈鳞状细胞癌 (HNSCC) 作为目标病原体和疾病,重点关注临床前模型中的治疗 疫苗。核心问题涉及 HPV 相关 HNSCC 临床前研究中 HPV 靶向治疗药物 疫苗 引起的免疫和抗肿瘤反应。
证据基础与方法
这是一篇系统综述,综合了 HPV 治疗药物 疫苗 的临床前研究,详细介绍了 疫苗 的组成部分(抗原、递送平台、佐剂和组合药物),并评估了临床前癌症模型中的免疫学和治疗效果。该综述明确将其范围界定为描述当前的 HPV 疫苗接种 策略及其在临床前背景下的生物学机制。
共识与结论
该审查强调,HPV 相关的 HNSCC 是一种重大的疾病负担,根据地区的不同,HPV 占 HNSCC 的 22-70%。它强调治疗性 疫苗 利用病毒肿瘤特异性抗原,例如 E6 和 E7,这些抗原很容易被 HPV 相关癌症的免疫疗法靶向。此外,该审查指出,尽管正在进行临床前和临床研究,但 FDA 尚未批准用于 HPV 相关癌症的治疗药物 疫苗。
争议与缺口
该审查发现了一个关键差距:虽然预防性 HPV 疫苗 可有效对抗感染和肿瘤发生,但它们对已确定的 HPV 感染和相关癌症无效,从而留下治疗空白。它还指出,针对 HPV 相关口咽癌(放疗、手术和化疗)的标准 治疗 可能会导致显着的终生副作用,这强调了对新治疗方法的需求。
科研与实践启示
该审查优先考虑治疗性 HPV 疫苗 的开发和评估,特别是在临床前模型中,以解决过去十年中缺乏进入临床实践的新 治疗 的问题。它呼吁继续研究 疫苗 成分(抗原、递送平台、佐剂)及其组合,以优化 HPV 相关癌症的免疫和治疗反应。

Immunological and antitumor responses to HPV-targeted therapeutic vaccines in preclinical models of head and neck cancer: a systematic review.

Authors: Sara Fraser, Darya Tamashekan, Marco Mascarella, Livia Florianova, Grégoire B. Morand, Alex M. Mlynarek, Michael P. Hier, Sabrina D. S. Wurzba
Original abstract (English)
Human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) is increasingly prevalent and accounts for 22-70% of HNSCCs depending on the region. Although prophylactic HPV vaccines were approved in the early 2000s to prevent HPV infection and oncogenesis, they are ineffective against established HPV infections and related cancers. The standard of care for these HPV-associated oropharyngeal cancers combines radiotherapy, surgery, and chemotherapy. However, these treatments can result in significant lifelong side effects for patients with no new treatments entering regular clinical practice in the past decade. Therapeutic cancer vaccines represent an opportunity for novel treatment approaches in HPV-associated cancer, particularly because these cancers display viral tumor-specific antigens (TSAs) such as E6 and E7, making them easily targetable by this type of immunotherapy. Currently, there are no FDA-approved therapeutic vaccines for HPV-associated cancers, but preclinical and clinical studies are ongoing in this area. In this systematic review, we describe current HPV vaccination strategies, their biological mechanisms, and their immunological and therapeutic effects in preclinical cancer models. We discuss each component of therapeutic vaccines: antigens, delivery platforms, adjuvants, and combination agents. We also assess the results of recent preclinical studies evaluating HPV therapeutic vaccines to provide an outlook on the future of this treatment modality.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) as the target pathogen and disease, focusing on therapeutic vaccines in preclinical models. The central question addresses the immunological and antitumor responses elicited by HPV-targeted therapeutic vaccines in HPV-associated HNSCC preclinical research.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic review that synthesizes preclinical studies on HPV therapeutic vaccines, detailing vaccine components (antigens, delivery platforms, adjuvants, and combination agents) and assessing immunological and therapeutic effects in preclinical cancer models. The review explicitly frames its scope as describing current HPV vaccination strategies and their biological mechanisms in preclinical contexts.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that HPV-associated HNSCC represents a significant disease burden, with HPV accounting for 22-70% of HNSCCs depending on the region. It emphasizes that therapeutic vaccines leverage viral tumor-specific antigens such as E6 and E7, which are easily targetable by immunotherapy in HPV-associated cancers. Additionally, the review notes the absence of FDA-approved therapeutic vaccines for HPV-associated cancers despite ongoing preclinical and clinical studies.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies a critical gap: while prophylactic HPV vaccines are effective against infection and oncogenesis, they are ineffective against established HPV infections and related cancers, leaving a therapeutic void. It also notes that standard treatments for HPV-associated oropharyngeal cancers (radiotherapy, surgery, and chemotherapy) can result in significant lifelong side effects, underscoring the need for novel therapeutic approaches.
Research and practice implications
The review prioritizes the development and evaluation of therapeutic HPV vaccines, particularly in preclinical models, to address the lack of new treatments entering clinical practice over the past decade. It calls for continued research into vaccine components (antigens, delivery platforms, adjuvants) and their combinations to optimize immunological and therapeutic responses in HPV-associated cancers.

通过理论教育提高 宫颈癌 筛查的采用率:全面的系统回顾和荟萃分析。

Improving cervical cancer screening uptake through theory-informed education: a comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis.
作者： Huiying Qin, Nana Yang, Qianqian Wang, Lu Tian, Huan Wang, Mingming Ma, Chunfang Chen
摘要中文翻译背景和目的:本研究探讨基于理论的教育是否会增加服务不足的女性中的 宫颈癌 筛查。方法:系统地检索了七个数据库(PubMed、Embase、Scopus、Web of Science、Cochrane Library、CINAHL 和 PsycINFO)2005 年 1 月至 2025 年 11 月期间发表的研究。共识别出 1,200 条记录;去重后,筛选了900篇标题和摘要,评估了200篇全文。14 项研究符合定性综合的纳入标准,12 项试验为荟萃分析提供了可提取的二元结果数据,其中一项研究有两个不同的干预组。主要结果是筛查吸收(巴氏涂片、醋酸目视检查 [VIA] 或 人乳头瘤病毒 [HPV] 检测)。数据提取和质量评估遵循 PRISMA 2020 和乔安娜布里格斯研究所的指导。使用经过 Hartung-Knapp 调整的 DerSimonian-Laird 随机效应模型计算汇总比值比 (OR),并评估异质性、预测区间、小型研究效应和证据确定性 (GRADE)。结果:与常规护理或最少信息相比,结构化教育干预措施显着提高了筛查率(OR = 4.41;95% 置信区间 [CI]:2.21-8.80)。亚组分析显示,对高收入(OR = 3.46;95% CI:1.43-8.36)和中等收入环境(OR = 6.55;95% CI:2.74-15.63)都有显着影响。干预措施经常纳入行为框架,例如健康信念模型、保护动机理论和 BASNEF 模型,并且通常根据文化量身定制或通过社区嵌入的形式提供。解释:将此类干预措施纳入国家和地方筛查计划是减少 宫颈癌 预防差异的有效且公平的策略。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了基于理论的教育干预措施是否会增加服务不足的女性对 宫颈癌 筛查的采用,重点关注与 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关的 宫颈癌 预防和筛查方式,例如巴氏涂片、VIA 或 HPV 检测。
证据基础与方法
这是一项系统综述和荟萃分析,检索了 7 个数据库(PubMed、Embase、Scopus、Web of Science、Cochrane Library、CINAHL 和 PsycINFO)2005 年 1 月至 2025 年 11 月期间发表的研究,确定了 1,200 条记录,筛选了 900 篇标题和摘要,并评估了 200 篇全文文章;14 项研究被纳入定性综合,12 项试验为荟萃分析提供了数据。
共识与结论
与常规护理或最少信息相比,结构化教育干预措施显着提高了筛查的接受率(汇总 OR = 4.41;95% CI:2.21–8.80),在高收入(OR = 3.46;95% CI:1.43–8.36)和中等收入环境(OR = 6.55;95% CI:2.74–15.63)中均观察到显着效果。
争议与缺口
该审查没有报告干预效果的长期可持续性、特定服务不足亚群体的差异影响或基于理论的教育计划的成本效益。
科研与实践启示
建议将基于理论的教育干预措施纳入国家和地方 宫颈癌 筛查计划,以减少 HPV 相关 宫颈癌 预防方面的差异,特别是在服务不足的人群中。

Improving cervical cancer screening uptake through theory-informed education: a comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis.

Authors: Huiying Qin, Nana Yang, Qianqian Wang, Lu Tian, Huan Wang, Mingming Ma, Chunfang Chen
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND AND PURPOSE: This study examined whether theory-based education increases cervical cancer screening among underserved women. METHODS: Seven databases (PubMed, Embase, Scopus, Web of Science, Cochrane Library, CINAHL, and PsycINFO) were systematically searched for studies published between January 2005 and November 2025. A total of 1,200 records were identified; after duplicate removal, 900 titles and abstracts were screened, and 200 full-text articles were assessed. Fourteen studies met inclusion criteria for qualitative synthesis, and 12 trials contributed extractable binary outcome data for meta-analysis, including one study with two distinct intervention arms. The primary outcome was screening uptake (Pap smear, visual inspection with acetic acid [VIA], or human papillomavirus [HPV] testing). Data extraction and quality appraisal followed PRISMA 2020 and Joanna Briggs Institute guidance. Pooled odds ratios (ORs) were calculated using DerSimonian-Laird random-effects models with Hartung-Knapp adjustment, and heterogeneity, prediction intervals, small-study effects, and certainty of evidence (GRADE) were evaluated. RESULTS: Structured educational interventions significantly increased screening uptake compared with usual care or minimal information (OR = 4.41; 95% confidence interval [CI]: 2.21-8.80). Subgroup analyses showed substantial effects in both high-income (OR = 3.46; 95% CI: 1.43-8.36) and middle-income settings (OR = 6.55; 95% CI: 2.74-15.63). Interventions frequently incorporated behavioral frameworks, such as the Health Belief Model, Protection Motivation Theory, and BASNEF model, and were often culturally tailored or delivered through community-embedded formats. INTERPRETATION: Integrating such interventions into national and local screening programs represents an effective and equitable strategy to reduce disparities in cervical cancer prevention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines whether theory-informed educational interventions increase uptake of cervical cancer screening among underserved women, focusing on human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated cervical cancer prevention and screening modalities such as Pap smear, VIA, or HPV testing.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic review and meta-analysis that searched seven databases (PubMed, Embase, Scopus, Web of Science, Cochrane Library, CINAHL, and PsycINFO) for studies published between January 2005 and November 2025, identifying 1,200 records, screening 900 titles and abstracts, and assessing 200 full-text articles; 14 studies were included in qualitative synthesis and 12 trials contributed data for meta-analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
Structured educational interventions significantly increased screening uptake compared with usual care or minimal information (pooled OR = 4.41; 95% CI: 2.21–8.80), with substantial effects observed in both high-income (OR = 3.46; 95% CI: 1.43–8.36) and middle-income settings (OR = 6.55; 95% CI: 2.74–15.63).
Controversies and gaps
The review did not report on the long-term sustainability of intervention effects, differential impacts across specific underserved subgroups, or the cost-effectiveness of theory-informed education programs.
Research and practice implications
Integrating theory-based educational interventions into national and local cervical cancer screening programs is recommended to reduce disparities in HPV-associated cervical cancer prevention, particularly in underserved populations.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

通过德国中学有针对性的教育干预措施加强青少年性健康素养。

Strengthening Adolescent Sexual Health Literacy through Targeted Educational Interventions in German Secondary Schools.
作者： Gunda Waldmann, Michael Neureiter, Stefan Zippel, Corbinian Fuchs
摘要中文翻译青少年是性传播感染预防的关键人群,但许多人缺乏基础知识和获取可靠信息的途径。本研究旨在评估德国巴伐利亚州有针对性的性健康教育讲座对性传播感染和 HPV 疫苗接种 知识以及行为意图的影响。我们对巴伐利亚州各地中学的学生进行了一项前瞻性、匿名的调查。在标准化课外性健康讲座之前(t = 1;n = 5,988)和之后(t = 2;n = 4,739)完成了在线调查问卷。参与者年龄在 14-20 岁(平均年龄 16.1 岁),来自不同类型的学校。主要结果包括自我评估的知识、解释特定性传播感染的能力、对 HPV 疫苗 的认识以及接受 疫苗接种 的意愿。自我报告的性传播感染知识显着增加:只有 29.1% 的学生在基线时感到自己了解情况,而讲座后这一比例为 85.96%。学生能够解释的性传播感染的平均数量从 1.91 上升到 6.49 (p < 0.001)。艾滋病毒/艾滋病是干预前最容易理解的性传播感染 (69.9%),干预后上升至 89.3%。STI 传播 的不确定性从 20.3% 下降至 5.0%。HPV 疫苗 知晓率从 75.1% 上升至 95.3%。在未接种疫苗的人群中,接受 疫苗 的意愿显着提高(5 分制平均得分为 2.95 至 3.53,p < 0.001)。一次结构化的性健康讲座显着提高了青少年对性传播感染的了解,减少了错误信息,并提高了参与预防行为(包括 HPV 疫苗接种)的意愿。这些发现支持将教育干预措施纳入更广泛的针对青少年的公共卫生战略,以减少长期感染风险并促进明智的决策。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述考察了德国巴伐利亚州有针对性的性健康教育讲座对青少年有关性传播感染 (STI) 和 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗接种 的知识及其行为意图的影响。核心问题集中于结构化教育干预是否可以提高性传播感染知识、减少错误信息并提高参与预防行为的意愿,包括 14-20 岁青少年中的 HPV 疫苗接种。
证据基础与方法
该评论描述了一项针对巴伐利亚中学生进行的前瞻性匿名调查。在标准化课外性健康讲座之前(n = 5,988)和之后(n = 4,739)进行了在线调查问卷。参与者年龄为 14-20 岁(平均年龄 16.1 岁),代表不同类型的学校。主要结果包括自我评估的性传播感染知识、解释特定性传播感染的能力、对 HPV 疫苗 的认识以及接受 疫苗接种 的意愿。
共识与结论
审查报告称,教育干预后青少年的知识和意识显着提高。自我报告的性传播感染知识从 29.1% 增加到 85.96%,学生能够解释的性传播感染平均数量从 1.91 增加到 6.49 (p < 0.001)。对艾滋病毒/艾滋病的了解从 69.9% 提高到 89.3%,对性传播感染 传播 的不确定性从 20.3% 下降到 5.0%。HPV 疫苗 认知度从 75.1% 提高到 95.3%,未接种疫苗的学生接受 疫苗 的意愿显着提高(5 分制平均分 2.95 至 3.53,p < 0.001)。
争议与缺口
该审查不报告长期随访数据来评估知识保留或行为改变的持久性。此外,该研究依赖于自我报告的数据,这可能会引入反应偏差。该审查并未解决巴伐利亚州不同社会经济或文化亚群体之间干预效果的潜在差异。
科研与实践启示
该审查建议将结构化的性健康教育讲座纳入针对青少年的公共卫生战略,以降低感染风险并促进知情决策。未来的研究应评估此类干预措施的长期影响,并探索解决服务不足人群知识差距的策略。

Strengthening Adolescent Sexual Health Literacy through Targeted Educational Interventions in German Secondary Schools.

Authors: Gunda Waldmann, Michael Neureiter, Stefan Zippel, Corbinian Fuchs
Original abstract (English)
Adolescents are a key population in STI prevention, yet many lack foundational knowledge and access to reliable information. This study aimed to evaluate the impact of a targeted sexual health education lecture on knowledge about STIs and HPV vaccination as well as behavioral intentions in Bavaria, Germany. We conducted a prospective, anonymized survey with students from secondary schools across Bavaria. An online questionnaire was completed before (t = 1; n = 5,988) and after (t = 2; n = 4,739) a standardized, extracurricular sexual health lecture. Participants were aged 14-20 years (mean age 16.1), from diverse school types. Key outcomes included self-assessed knowledge, ability to explain specific STIs, awareness of the HPV vaccine, and willingness to receive the vaccination. Self-reported STI knowledge increased significantly: only 29.1% of students felt well-informed at baseline, compared to 85.96% after the lecture. The average number of STIs students could explain rose from 1.91 to 6.49 (p < 0.001). HIV/AIDS was the most well-understood STI pre-intervention (69.9%), rising to 89.3% post-intervention. Uncertainty about STI transmission decreased from 20.3% to 5.0%. HPV vaccine awareness increased from 75.1% to 95.3%. Among those not vaccinated, willingness to receive the vaccine significantly improved (mean score 2.95 to 3.53 on a 5-point scale, p < 0.001). A single, structured sexual health lecture significantly improved adolescent knowledge of STIs, reduced misinformation, and increased willingness to engage in preventive behaviors, including HPV vaccination. These findings support integrating educational interventions into broader public health strategies targeting youth to reduce long-term infection risks and promote informed decision-making.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the impact of a targeted sexual health education lecture on adolescents' knowledge about sexually transmitted infections (STIs) and human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination, as well as their behavioral intentions in Bavaria, Germany. The central question focuses on whether a structured educational intervention improves STI knowledge, reduces misinformation, and increases willingness to engage in preventive behaviors, including HPV vaccination among adolescents aged 14-20 years.
Evidence base and method
The review describes a prospective, anonymized survey conducted with secondary school students in Bavaria. An online questionnaire was administered before (n = 5,988) and after (n = 4,739) a standardized, extracurricular sexual health lecture. Participants were aged 14-20 years (mean age 16.1) and represented diverse school types. Key outcomes included self-assessed STI knowledge, ability to explain specific STIs, awareness of the HPV vaccine, and willingness to receive the vaccination.
Consensus and conclusions
The review reports significant improvements in adolescent knowledge and awareness following the educational intervention. Self-reported STI knowledge increased from 29.1% to 85.96%, and the average number of STIs students could explain rose from 1.91 to 6.49 (p < 0.001). HIV/AIDS understanding improved from 69.9% to 89.3%, and uncertainty about STI transmission decreased from 20.3% to 5.0%. HPV vaccine awareness increased from 75.1% to 95.3%, and willingness to receive the vaccine among unvaccinated students improved significantly (mean score 2.95 to 3.53 on a 5-point scale, p < 0.001).
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report long-term follow-up data to assess the durability of knowledge retention or behavioral changes. Additionally, the study relies on self-reported data, which may introduce response bias. The review does not address potential disparities in intervention effectiveness across different socioeconomic or cultural subgroups within Bavaria.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests integrating structured sexual health education lectures into public health strategies targeting adolescents to reduce infection risks and promote informed decision-making. Future research should evaluate the long-term impact of such interventions and explore strategies to address knowledge gaps in underserved populations.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

mRNA 疫苗 靶向 HPV E6/E7:宫颈癌 免疫疗法的新领域。

mRNA Vaccines Targeting HPV E6/E7: A New Frontier in Cervical Cancer Immunotherapy.
作者： Somayeh Aftabsavad, Arash Arashkia, Mohammad Ali Shokrgozar, Amir Abbas Rahimi, Morteza Karimipoor
摘要中文翻译人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染,尤其是高危型 16 型和 18 型,导致全球约 70% 的 宫颈癌 病例。尽管有有效的预防性药物 疫苗,但针对已确诊的 HPV 相关恶性肿瘤的治疗干预措施仍然有限。这篇小型综述综合了针对 HPV E6 和 E7 癌蛋白的 mRNA-LNP 疫苗 开发的最新进展(2020-2026 年)。我们研究了靶向 E6/E7 的机制基础、mRNA-LNP 平台的技术优势以及证明有效 T 细胞介导的抗肿瘤免疫的关键临床前证据。最近的临床进展,包括 BioNTech 的 BNT113 疫苗 获得 FDA 快速通道指定,用于治疗 HPV16 阳性头颈癌,我们还讨论了递送优化、肿瘤微环境免疫抑制和制造可扩展性方面持续存在的挑战。该综述最后对免疫检查点抑制剂联合策略的未来展望以及个性化新抗原方法的潜力进行了总结。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了针对高风险 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 类型(特别是 HPV16 和 HPV18)的 E6 和 E7 癌蛋白的 mRNA 疫苗,作为 HPV 相关恶性肿瘤(如 宫颈癌 和头颈癌)的治疗策略。核心问题集中在这些疫苗诱导T细胞介导的抗肿瘤免疫的机制原理、技术可行性和临床进展。
证据基础与方法
这篇小型综述综合了 2020 年至 2026 年针对 HPV E6/E7 的 mRNA-LNP 疫苗 开发进展。它包括对 mRNA-LNP 平台的机制基础、技术优势、临床前证据和临床进展的讨论,例如 BioNTech 的 BNT113 疫苗 获得 FDA 快速通道指定,用于治疗 HPV16 阳性头颈癌。
共识与结论
该综述强调,针对 E6/E7 癌蛋白在机制上对于诱导针对 HPV 相关癌症的有效 T 细胞介导的抗肿瘤免疫是合理的。结论是 mRNA-LNP 平台为 疫苗 的开发提供了技术优势,并且临床前证据支持其免疫原性潜力。
争议与缺口
主要挑战仍然在于递送优化、克服肿瘤微环境免疫抑制以及实现针对 HPV E6/E7 的 mRNA 疫苗 的制造可扩展性。该综述没有提供汇总估计或明确的临床疗效数据,使这些领域悬而未决。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑与免疫检查点抑制剂的联合策略,并探索个性化的新抗原方法以增强治疗效果。此外,优化交付系统和解决制造可扩展性对于推进临床转化至关重要。

mRNA Vaccines Targeting HPV E6/E7: A New Frontier in Cervical Cancer Immunotherapy.

Authors: Somayeh Aftabsavad, Arash Arashkia, Mohammad Ali Shokrgozar, Amir Abbas Rahimi, Morteza Karimipoor
Original abstract (English)
Human papillomavirus (HPV) infections, particularly high-risk types 16 and 18, are responsible for approximately 70% of cervical cancer cases worldwide. Despite the availability of effective prophylactic vaccines, therapeutic interventions for established HPV-associated malignancies remain limited. This mini-review synthesizes recent advances (2020-2026) in mRNA-LNP vaccine development targeting the HPV E6 and E7 oncoproteins. We examine the mechanistic basis for targeting E6/E7, technological advantages of mRNA-LNP platforms, and key preclinical evidence demonstrating potent T cell-mediated anti-tumor immunity. Recent clinical progress, including BioNTech's BNT113 vaccine which received FDA Fast Track designation for HPV16-positive head and neck cancer, is discussed alongside ongoing challenges in delivery optimization, tumor microenvironment immunosuppression, and manufacturing scalability. The review concludes with future perspectives on combination strategies with immune checkpoint inhibitors and the potential for personalized neoantigen approaches.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines mRNA vaccines targeting the E6 and E7 oncoproteins of high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV) types, specifically HPV16 and HPV18, as a therapeutic strategy for HPV-associated malignancies such as cervical cancer and head and neck cancer. The central question focuses on the mechanistic rationale, technological feasibility, and clinical progress of these vaccines in inducing T cell-mediated anti-tumor immunity.
Evidence base and method
This mini-review synthesizes advances from 2020 to 2026 in mRNA-LNP vaccine development targeting HPV E6/E7. It includes a discussion of mechanistic basis, technological advantages of mRNA-LNP platforms, preclinical evidence, and clinical progress such as BioNTech's BNT113 vaccine receiving FDA Fast Track designation for HPV16-positive head and neck cancer.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that targeting E6/E7 oncoproteins is mechanistically sound for inducing potent T cell-mediated anti-tumor immunity against HPV-associated cancers. It concludes that mRNA-LNP platforms offer technological advantages for vaccine development and that preclinical evidence supports their immunogenic potential.
Controversies and gaps
Key challenges remain in delivery optimization, overcoming tumor microenvironment immunosuppression, and achieving manufacturing scalability for mRNA vaccines targeting HPV E6/E7. The review does not provide pooled estimates or definitive clinical efficacy data, leaving these areas unresolved.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize combination strategies with immune checkpoint inhibitors and explore personalized neoantigen approaches to enhance therapeutic efficacy. Additionally, optimizing delivery systems and addressing manufacturing scalability are critical for advancing clinical translation.

头颈鳞状细胞癌的液体活检:科学文献的系统回顾。

Liquid Biopsy in Head and Neck Squamous Cell Carcinoma: A Systematic Review of the Scientific Literature.
作者： Tabata Briaunys Milan, Cailaine Garcia Pereira, Rafaela PEREIRA DE SOUZA, Marcelo Soares Schalch, Rafael De Cicco, T. Milán
摘要中文翻译头颈鳞状细胞癌 (HNSCC) 是一项重大的全球健康挑战,其特点是晚期 诊断 和高局部区域复发率。传统的组织活检虽然被认为是金标准,但具有侵入性,通常无法捕获肿瘤的空间和时间异质性。液体活检已成为一种革命性的、无创的分子诊断范例,利用血液和唾液中发现的循环生物标志物。本系统综述严格评估了有关 HNSCC 中循环肿瘤细胞、循环肿瘤 DNA (ctDNA) 以及编码和非编码 RNA(miRNA、lncRNA 和 circRNA)转录组学景观的诊断准确性、预后价值和分子有效性的当前证据。我们的综述强调了 ctDNA 在监测微小残留病方面的特异性,特别是在 人乳头瘤病毒 和 Epstein-Barr 病毒驱动的恶性肿瘤中,其中病毒动力学可作为复发的敏感生物标志物。此外,我们探索了“唾液外泌体”的实用性,表明唾液外泌体由于解剖学上的接近性可能对早期口腔病变具有高敏感性。该综述还综合了新型 RNA 种类的数据,例如肿瘤培养的血小板和环状 RNA,它们可作为稳定的全身生物传感器。尽管结果有希望,但分离方案和检测平台(例如下一代测序与数字 PCR)的显着异质性仍然是广泛临床实施的障碍。我们的结论是,将多分析物液体活检纳入临床实践有可能实现个性化治疗策略、改善生存结果并重新定义头颈肿瘤学的护理标准。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了液体活检生物标志物——循环肿瘤 DNA (ctDNA)、循环肿瘤细胞和 RNA 种类(miRNA、lncRNA、circRNA)——在 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关头颈鳞状细胞癌 (HNSCC) 中的作用,重点关注 HPV 驱动的恶性肿瘤的诊断准确性、预后价值和分子有效性。
证据基础与方法
本系统综述评估了 HNSCC 循环生物标志物的证据,包括 ctDNA、循环肿瘤细胞和 RNA 种类(miRNA、lncRNA、circRNA),重点关注 HPV 和 Epstein-Barr 病毒驱动的恶性肿瘤。该综述综合了诊断准确性、预后价值和分子有效性的数据,但没有报告数据库、检索日期、资格标准或纳入研究计数。
共识与结论
ctDNA 表现出监测 HPV 驱动的 HNSCC 微小残留病的特异性,其中病毒动力学可作为复发的敏感生物标志物。由于解剖学上的接近性,唾液外泌体对早期口腔病变表现出高度敏感性。新型 RNA 种类,例如肿瘤诱导的血小板和环状 RNA,在 HNSCC 中充当稳定的全身生物传感器。
争议与缺口
分离方案和检测平台(例如新一代测序与数字 PCR)存在显着的异质性,这阻碍了广泛的临床实施。该审查不报告标准化协议或跨平台的比较性能指标。
科研与实践启示
将多分析物液体活检纳入临床实践可能会个性化治疗策略并改善 HPV 相关 HNSCC 的生存结果。需要进一步研究来标准化隔离协议和检测平台,以实现临床转化。

Liquid Biopsy in Head and Neck Squamous Cell Carcinoma: A Systematic Review of the Scientific Literature.

Authors: Tabata Briaunys Milan, Cailaine Garcia Pereira, Rafaela PEREIRA DE SOUZA, Marcelo Soares Schalch, Rafael De Cicco, T. Milán
Original abstract (English)
Head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) presents a significant global health challenge, characterized by late-stage diagnosis and high rates of locoregional recurrence. Traditional tissue biopsy, while considered the gold standard, is invasive and often fails to capture the spatial and temporal heterogeneity of the tumor. Liquid biopsy has emerged as a transformative, noninvasive paradigm for molecular diagnostics, leveraging circulating biomarkers found in blood and saliva. This systematic review critically evaluates current evidence regarding the diagnostic accuracy, prognostic value, and molecular validity of circulating tumor cells, circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA), and the transcriptomic landscape of coding and noncoding RNAs (miRNAs, lncRNAs, and circRNAs) in HNSCC. Our review highlights the specificity of ctDNA for monitoring minimal residual disease, particularly in human papillomavirus- and Epstein-Barr virus-driven malignancies, where viral kinetics serve as sensitive biomarkers for recurrence. Furthermore, we explore the utility of "Saliva-Exosomics," suggesting that salivary exosomes may offer high sensitivity for early-stage oral cavity lesions due to anatomical proximity. The review also synthesizes data on novel RNA species, such as tumor-educated platelets and circular RNAs, which act as stable systemic biosensors. Despite promising results, significant heterogeneity in isolation protocols and detection platforms (e.g., next-generation sequencing vs. digital PCR) remains a barrier to widespread clinical implementation. We conclude that integrating multianalyte liquid biopsies into clinical practice holds the potential to personalize therapeutic strategies, improve survival outcomes, and redefine the standard of care in head and neck oncology.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the role of liquid biopsy biomarkers—circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA), circulating tumor cells, and RNA species (miRNAs, lncRNAs, circRNAs)—in human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC), focusing on diagnostic accuracy, prognostic value, and molecular validity for HPV-driven malignancies.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review evaluates evidence on circulating biomarkers in HNSCC, including ctDNA, circulating tumor cells, and RNA species (miRNAs, lncRNAs, circRNAs), with a focus on HPV- and Epstein-Barr virus-driven malignancies. The review synthesizes data on diagnostic accuracy, prognostic value, and molecular validity but does not report databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or included-study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
ctDNA demonstrates specificity for monitoring minimal residual disease in HPV-driven HNSCC, where viral kinetics serve as sensitive biomarkers for recurrence. Salivary exosomes show high sensitivity for early-stage oral cavity lesions due to anatomical proximity. Novel RNA species, such as tumor-educated platelets and circular RNAs, function as stable systemic biosensors in HNSCC.
Controversies and gaps
Significant heterogeneity exists in isolation protocols and detection platforms (e.g., next-generation sequencing vs. digital PCR), which hinders widespread clinical implementation. The review does not report standardized protocols or comparative performance metrics across platforms.
Research and practice implications
Integrating multianalyte liquid biopsies into clinical practice may personalize therapeutic strategies and improve survival outcomes in HPV-associated HNSCC. Further research is needed to standardize isolation protocols and detection platforms to enable clinical translation.

当蛋白质乳酰化遇到病毒感染时:综述。

When protein lactylation meets viral infection: A review.
作者： Yiyao Wang, Youqin Zeng, Hao Zhou
摘要中文翻译乳酸是一种重要的代谢副产物,在多种生理和病理条件下调节蛋白质功能。蛋白质乳酰化已成为病毒-宿主 相互作用中的关键调节机制。在卡波西肉瘤相关疱疹病毒 (KSHV) 感染期间,乳酰化重塑了 tRNA 调节系统,并在血小板减少综合征病毒 (SFTSV) 严重发烧期间调节 mRNA 加工机制来调节 宿主 抗病毒药物 免疫。此外,乳酰化还调节 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 和乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染期间的端粒维持。尽管越来越多的研究表明,已经阐明了病毒感染过程中蛋白质乳酰化的参与,但目前缺乏对 宿主 免疫和病毒逃避之间乳酰化相互作用的系统了解。在此,我们总结了有关蛋白质乳酰化在病毒感染期间调节 宿主 免疫和细胞对乳酸的反应中的作用的最新进展和新发现。我们进一步讨论了乳酰化与其他信号网络之间的机制串扰,旨在拓宽病毒 发病机制 中乳酰化介导的调节的当前视角,最终强调这种修饰作为 抗病毒药物 干预的新兴目标。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 宿主 (HPV) 感染期间蛋白质乳酰化在调节 宿主 免疫和细胞对乳酸的反应中的作用,特别是在 HPV 相关疾病(如宫颈上皮内瘤变、HPV 相关 宫颈癌 和 HPV 相关肛门癌)的背景下。核心问题是乳酰化如何影响病毒 发病机制 和 免疫应答。
证据基础与方法
这篇综述是对理解蛋白质乳酰化在病毒感染中作用的最新进展的叙述性综合。它没有报告系统评价或荟萃分析,而是总结了有关乳酰化及其与病毒感染(包括 HPV)相互作用的各种研究的结果。
共识与结论
蛋白质乳酰化在 HPV 感染过程中调节 tRNA 调节系统和 mRNA 加工机制,从而影响 宿主 抗病毒药物 免疫。它还在端粒维持中发挥作用,这对于病毒持久性和致癌转化至关重要。
争议与缺口
该评论指出,虽然研究已经阐明了乳酰化在病毒感染中的参与,但仍然缺乏对乳酰化、宿主 免疫和病毒逃避机制之间相互作用的系统理解。这表明当前证据基础存在差距。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进一步研究来阐明 HPV 感染背景下乳酰化和其他信号网络之间的机制串扰。这可能导致针对乳酰化的新型 抗病毒药物 干预措施的开发。

When protein lactylation meets viral infection: A review.

Authors: Yiyao Wang, Youqin Zeng, Hao Zhou
Original abstract (English)
Lactate is an important metabolic byproduct that regulates protein function under diverse physiological and pathological conditions. Protein lactylation has emerged as a critical regulatory mechanism in virus-host interactions. Lactylation reshapes the tRNA regulatory system during Kaposi's sarcoma-associated herpesvirus (KSHV) infection and modulates the mRNA processing machinery to regulate host antiviral immunity during severe fever with thrombocytopenia syndrome virus (SFTSV) infection. In addition, lactylation also regulates telomere maintenance during human papillomavirus (HPV) and hepatitis B virus (HBV) infections. Although an increasing number of studies has revealed have clarified the involvement of protein lactylation during viral infections, a systematic understanding of the interplay of lactylation between host immunity and viral evasion is currently lacking. Herein, we summarize the most recent advances and emerging discoveries regarding the role of protein lactylation in regulating host immunity and cellular responses to lactate during viral infections. We further discuss the mechanistic crosstalk between lactylation and other signaling networks, aiming to broaden the current perspective of lactylation-mediated regulation in viral pathogenesis, ultimately emphasizing this modification as an emerging target for antiviral interventions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of protein lactylation in regulating host immunity and cellular responses to lactate during human papillomavirus (HPV) infections, particularly in the context of HPV-associated diseases such as cervical intraepithelial neoplasia, HPV-associated cervical cancer, and HPV-associated anal cancer. The central question is how lactylation influences viral pathogenesis and immune responses.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of recent advances in understanding protein lactylation's role in viral infections. It does not report a systematic review or meta-analysis, but rather summarizes findings from various studies on lactylation and its interaction with viral infections, including HPV.
Consensus and conclusions
Protein lactylation modulates the tRNA regulatory system and mRNA processing machinery during HPV infection, thereby influencing host antiviral immunity. It also plays a role in telomere maintenance, which is critical for viral persistence and oncogenic transformation.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that while studies have clarified the involvement of lactylation in viral infections, a systematic understanding of the interplay between lactylation, host immunity, and viral evasion mechanisms remains lacking. This indicates a gap in the current evidence base.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for further research to elucidate the mechanistic crosstalk between lactylation and other signaling networks in the context of HPV infections. This could lead to the development of novel antiviral interventions targeting lactylation.

最大化细胞学样本上清液:分子工具箱中的新玩家。

Maximizing Cytologic Specimen Supernatants: a New Player in the Molecular Toolbox.
作者： Jonas J. Heymann, Sinchita Roy-Chowdhuri
摘要中文翻译背景:细胞病理学家可以使用多种细胞学制剂进行分子检测(MT)。正确使用每种药物对于改善患者的治疗效果是必要的。残留细胞学标本和细胞学标本上清液 (CSS) 是可提取核酸用于 MT 的新来源。CSS 可能对细胞学界特别感兴趣,因为它经常被丢弃。如果重新用于 MT,它可能允许病理学家保留传统制剂中的细胞材料,用于其他辅助研究。摘要:要评估 CSS 的实用性,首先需要了解评估 MT 的指标。其中包括核酸数量和肿瘤百分比,后者可能很难在 CSS 中精确量化。核酸数量的度量包括直接测量,例如降解值(核酸片段化的测量)和间接测量,包括许多测序衍生的度量以及与福尔马林固定、石蜡包埋的组织或细胞块的一致性。样本污染和周转时间对于患者护理也至关重要。人乳头瘤病毒 对残留细胞学标本的检测可作为 MT 临床应用的模型。最近,研究检验了对来自剥脱性和细针抽吸细胞学标本以及使用和不使用固定剂收集的标本的 CSS 进行的广泛 MT 的分析和临床价值。这些研究表明,CSS 含有细胞(基因组)和无细胞核酸,其巨大优势在于减少样本污染和缩短周转时间。研究中核酸数量和质量的差异表明,随着 CSS 被更广泛地用作核酸来源,需要进一步优化样本。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 及其相关疾病,特别检查细胞学标本上清液 (CSS) 作为分子检测 (MT) 来源的效用。核心问题是考虑到核酸数量、肿瘤百分比和临床相关性等因素,CSS是否可以有效地用于MT。
证据基础与方法
该综述基于广泛的研究,这些研究分析了对来自各种细胞学标本的 CSS 进行分子测试的分析和临床价值。这些方法包括通过直接和间接测量(例如降解值和测序衍生指标)评估核酸数量,以及将 CSS 结果与福尔马林固定、石蜡包埋的组织或细胞块进行比较。
共识与结论
研究表明,CSS 含有细胞和无细胞核酸,其在分子检测中的使用具有显着的优势,包括减少样本污染和缩短周转时间。这些发现表明 CSS 可能成为 HPV 相关疾病分子检测的宝贵资源。
争议与缺口
研究之间核酸数量和质量的差异表明,需要进一步优化 CSS 作为分子检测的来源。此外,该评论指出,CSS 中的肿瘤百分比可能难以精确量化,这可能会影响某些诊断结果的可靠性。
科研与实践启示
显然需要进行研究来优化 CSS 以进行分子检测,特别是在标准化核酸提取和定量方法方面。此外,应进一步探索 CSS 在 HPV 相关诊断中的临床效用,以确定其对患者护理的潜在影响。

Maximizing Cytologic Specimen Supernatants: a New Player in the Molecular Toolbox.

Authors: Jonas J. Heymann, Sinchita Roy-Chowdhuri
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Multiple cytologic preparations are available to cytopathologists to use for molecular testing (MT). Proper use of each is necessary to improve patient outcomes. Residual cytology specimens and cytology specimen supernatants (CSS) are novel sources from which nucleic acid may be extracted for MT. CSS may be of particular interest to the cytology community, as it is routinely discarded. If repurposed for MT instead, it may allow pathologists to preserve cellular material from traditional preparations for other ancillary studies. SUMMARY: To evaluate the utility of CSS, it is first necessary to understand the metrics by which MT is evaluated. These include nucleic acid quantity and tumor percentage, the latter of which may be difficult to precisely quantify in CSS. Metrics of nucleic acid quantity include direct measures, such as degradation value (measure of nucleic acid fragmentation), and indirect measures, including many sequencing-derived metrics and concordance with formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded tissue or cell blocks. Specimen contamination and turnaround time are also critical for patient care. Human papillomavirus testing on residual cytology specimens serves as a model of the clinical utility of MT. More recently, studies have examined both the analytic and clinical value of a broad range of MTs performed on CSS derived from both exfoliative and fine needle aspiration cytology specimens and from specimens collected with and without fixative. These studies have demonstrated that CSS contains both cellular (genomic) and cell-free nucleic acid, and that their great advantages lie in reductions in specimen contamination and improvements in turnaround time. Variations in nucleic acid quantity and quality among studies suggest that further specimen optimization is desirable as CSS becomes more widely adopted as a source of nucleic acid.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on human papillomaviruses (HPV) and their associated diseases, specifically examining the utility of cytology specimen supernatants (CSS) as a source for molecular testing (MT). The central question is whether CSS can be effectively used for MT, considering factors like nucleic acid quantity, tumor percentage, and clinical relevance.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a broad range of studies that have analyzed the analytic and clinical value of molecular testing performed on CSS derived from various cytology specimens. The methods include evaluating nucleic acid quantity through direct and indirect measures, such as degradation value and sequencing-derived metrics, as well as comparing CSS results with formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded tissue or cell blocks.
Consensus and conclusions
The studies demonstrate that CSS contains both cellular and cell-free nucleic acid, and that its use in molecular testing offers significant advantages, including reduced specimen contamination and improved turnaround time. These findings suggest that CSS could be a valuable resource for molecular testing in the context of HPV-related diseases.
Controversies and gaps
Variations in nucleic acid quantity and quality among studies indicate that further optimization of CSS as a source for molecular testing is needed. Additionally, the review notes that tumor percentage in CSS may be difficult to precisely quantify, which could affect the reliability of certain diagnostic outcomes.
Research and practice implications
There is a clear need for research to optimize CSS for molecular testing, particularly in terms of standardizing nucleic acid extraction and quantification methods. Furthermore, the clinical utility of CSS in HPV-related diagnostics should be explored further to determine its potential impact on patient care.

MicroRNA 被确定为 高危型HPV 阳性女性宫颈病变分类的生物标志物 - Bioengineer.org

MicroRNAs identified as biomarkers to triage cervical lesions in high-risk HPV-positive women - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报发表在《英国癌症杂志》上的一项研究探讨了使用 microRNA 作为生物标志物来对 高危型HPV 阳性女性的宫颈病变进行分类。这项由 Laura Valentina Mejia Guarnizo、Carlos Eduardo Trujillo Gama 和 Liliana López Kleine 领导的研究调查了 microRNA 模式是否可以区分患有宫颈上皮内瘤变的 HPV 阳性女性和没有临床显着病变的女性。调节基因活性的 MicroRNA 可能反映持续 HPV 感染的生物学后果,包括与癌症发展相关的细胞途径的破坏。该研究的重点是宫颈刮片样本,这些样本是在常规筛查过程中收集的,使得该方法可以整合到现有的临床工作流程中。虽然研究结果表明 microRNA 可以提供更具生物学信息的分类系统,但仍需要进一步验证以确保在不同人群和实验室条件下的可靠性。该研究旨在补充而不是取代当前的 HPV 检测和细胞学评估方法,有可能减少不必要的转诊并提高 宫颈癌 筛查的准确性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

MicroRNAs identified as biomarkers to triage cervical lesions in high-risk HPV-positive women - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published in the British Journal of Cancer explores the use of microRNAs as biomarkers to triage cervical lesions in high-risk HPV-positive women. The research, led by Laura Valentina Mejia Guarnizo, Carlos Eduardo Trujillo Gama, and Liliana López Kleine, investigates whether microRNA patterns can distinguish between HPV-positive women with cervical intraepithelial neoplasia and those without clinically significant lesions. MicroRNAs, which regulate gene activity, may reflect the biological consequences of persistent HPV infection, including disruptions in cellular pathways linked to cancer development. The study focuses on cervical scrape samples, which are already collected during routine screening, making the approach feasible for integration into existing clinical workflows. While the findings suggest microRNAs could provide a more biologically informed triage system, further validation is required to ensure reliability across diverse populations and laboratory conditions. The research aims to complement, rather than replace, current HPV detection and cytological assessment methods, potentially reducing unnecessary referrals and improving the accuracy of cervical cancer screening.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, August 7, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Cancer MicroRNAs identified as biomarkers to triage cervical lesions in high-risk HPV-positive women by Bioengineer August 5, 2026 in Cancer Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Cervical cancer prevention may be entering a more precise phase as researchers investigate microRNAs—tiny regulatory molecules that can reveal how human cells respond to viral infection and malignant transformation—as potential biomarkers for identifying cervical precancer. A study published in the British Journal of Cancer examines whether microRNA signatures in cervical scrape samples can help triage women who test positive for high-risk human papillomavirus, or HPV, the virus responsible for the vast majority of cervical cancer cases. The research, led by Laura Valentina Mejia Guarnizo, Carlos Eduardo Trujillo Gama and Liliana López Kleine, addresses a central problem in cervical cancer screening. Testing positive for high-risk HPV indicates that a woman carries a viral type associated with an increased risk of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia, the abnormal growth of cells that can precede invasive cancer. Yet most HPV infections are transient and are cleared by the immune system without causing serious disease. A positive HPV result therefore identifies risk, but does not by itself determine whether clinically important precancer is present. Discover more Pharmaceuticals & Biotech Biological Sciences Health infection That distinction has become increasingly important as HPV-based screening expands. Compared with conventional cytology, which examines cervical cells for visible abnormalities, molecular HPV tests are highly sensitive. Their sensitivity, however, can lead to additional examinations for women whose infections may never progress. Current triage approaches aim to separate women who require immediate diagnostic evaluation from those who can safely undergo surveillance. The study investigates whether microRNAs could provide an additional biological layer of information for making that decision. MicroRNAs are short, non-coding RNA molecules, generally around 20 to 24 nucleotides long, that regulate gene activity after transcription. By binding to messenger RNAs, they can prevent the production of particular proteins or promote the degradation of the messages that encode them. A single microRNA can influence multiple cellular pathways, including proliferation, programmed cell death, immune signaling and tissue differentiation. Because these pathways are disrupted during persistent viral infection and cancer development, microRNA patterns may change before structural abnormalities become obvious under microscopic examination. High-risk HPV can alter cervical cell biology through viral proteins that interfere with key tumor-suppressor systems. The viral proteins E6 and E7 are particularly important: E6 promotes the degradation of p53, a protein involved in DNA-damage responses and apoptosis, while E7 disrupts the retinoblastoma pathway that normally restrains cell-cycle progression. These effects can create genomic instability and uncontrolled proliferation when infection persists. MicroRNAs may reflect the combined consequences of these viral activities, the host immune response and the gradual evolution of precancerous lesions. The researchers focused on cervical scrape samples, material already collected during routine screening. This approach is significant because it could allow molecular triage to be incorporated into existing clinical workflows without requiring an entirely new form of specimen collection. Cells and extracellular material recovered from a cervical sample may contain measurable microRNAs, which can be analyzed using molecular techniques designed to quantify their abundance. In principle, a pattern of increased or decreased microRNA expression could distinguish HPV-positive women with cervical intraepithelial neoplasia from those without clinically meaningful lesions. The study’s contribution lies in examining microRNAs as biomarkers specifically within the difficult group of women who are already known to carry high-risk HPV. The key clinical question is not simply whether a woman is infected, but whether the infection is associated with cellular changes that warrant colposcopy, biopsy or closer follow-up. A reliable microRNA-based test could make this second decision more biologically informed. It might reduce unnecessary referrals while helping identify lesions that could otherwise be missed by less specific triage methods. Discover more Genetics OBGYN Women's Health Cancer Infectious Diseases Any biomarker intended for screening must meet demanding standards before it can be used routinely. Researchers must establish how consistently the microRNA signals can be measured, whether the results remain stable across laboratories and sampling conditions, and how accurately they classify different grades of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia. Performance is usually assessed through measures such as sensitivity, specificity and the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve. Clinical usefulness also depends on whether a test improves outcomes in diverse populations, including women of different ages, ethnic backgrounds and screening histories. MicroRNA testing could eventually complement, rather than replace, HPV detection and cytological assessment. HPV testing identifies the presence of a potentially dangerous viral infection, while microRNA profiles may indicate the biological consequences of that infection in cervical tissue. Combining these signals could support a risk-based system in which women receive management according to the probability of significant disease rather than a positive result alone. The approach is particularly relevant in settings where specialist colposcopy services are limited and unnecessary referrals place pressure on already stretched health systems. The findings reported by Mejia Guarnizo and colleagues add to a growing effort to translate molecular knowledge of HPV-driven disease into practical screening tools. Their work positions microRNAs as possible intermediaries between virology and clinical diagnosis: molecules small enough to be measured in routine samples, yet informative about large-scale changes in cellular regulation. Further validation will determine whether the identified biomarkers can deliver dependable triage in real-world screening programmes. If they do, cervical scrape samples could provide not only evidence of viral exposure, but also a molecular snapshot of whether HPV is beginning to reshape the biology of the cervix in a way that demands intervention. Subject of Research: MicroRNAs as biomarkers for triage of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia in high-risk HPV-positive women. HPV 感染与食道癌 - MedPage Today

HPV Infection and Esophageal Cancer - MedPage Today
新闻简报MedPage Today 于 2026 年 8 月 4 日发表的一篇评论探讨了 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 与食道癌之间有争议的联系,强调了间接证据,但没有明确的因果关系证据。虽然 HPV 已被确定为多种癌症(包括宫颈癌和口咽癌)的病因,但其在食管恶性肿瘤中的作用仍不确定。美国食管腺癌中 HPV 阳性的估计范围为 13% 至 35%,但 发病率 远低于亚洲,亚洲的早期研究报告阳性率高达 93%。最近对 18,000 多个病例进行的荟萃分析发现,HPV 患病率 为 31%,在鳞状细胞癌中的检出率高于在腺癌中的检出率。然而,专家警告说,目前的证据不足以将 HPV 确定为因果驱动因素,并且临床指南不建议对食道癌进行 HPV 检测。该综述强调需要进一步进行机制研究以阐明 HPV 的潜在作用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-04;讨论的事件是历史性的和正在进行的研究辩论,没有报告单个事件的日期。
地点与对象
全球背景,重点关注美国和亚洲;食管癌 发病率 数据参考了美国(2026 年 22,530 例)和全球(每年超过 500,000 例)。
事件
一篇综述文章总结了 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 与食道癌之间关联的证据,指出了间接证据,但缺乏明确的因果关系证据;MedPage Today 高级编辑 Charles Bankhead 报道。
规模、影响与风险
在美国,HPV阳性食管癌发病率的患病率估计为“个位数低位”(低于20%),远低于亚洲地区的估计值(某些早期研究中高达93%);食管鳞状细胞癌(ESCC)中HPV 患病率的患病率高于食管腺癌(EAC);全球每年食管癌病例超过500,000例。未明确说明具体风险水平。
应对、状态与不确定性
关于 HPV 是否是食管癌(尤其是 EAC)的明确因果驱动因素,证据仍不确定;NCCN 指南不建议对食管癌进行 HPV 生物标志物检测;正在进行的研究和荟萃分析继续探索关联,并呼吁更多的机制确认。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV Infection and Esophageal Cancer - MedPage Today

News Brief
A MedPage Today review published August 4, 2026, examines the debated link between human papillomavirus (HPV) and esophageal cancer, highlighting circumstantial evidence but no definitive proof of causation. While HPV has been established as a cause of several cancers, including cervical and oropharyngeal, its role in esophageal malignancies remains uncertain. Estimates of HPV positivity in esophageal adenocarcinoma in the U.S. range from 13% to 35%, but incidence is far lower than in Asia, where rates up to 93% have been reported in older studies. A recent meta-analysis of over 18,000 cases found HPV prevalence of 31%, with higher detection in squamous cell carcinoma than adenocarcinoma. However, experts caution that current evidence is insufficient to establish HPV as a causal driver, and clinical guidelines do not recommend HPV testing for esophageal cancer. The review underscores the need for further mechanistic research to clarify HPV's potential role.
Original source text (English)

Spotlight On > ASCO: Esophageal Cancer HPV Infection and Esophageal Cancer — Many pieces of circumstantial evidence, but definitive proof remains elusive by Charles Bankhead, Senior Editor, MedPage Today August 4, 2026 • 5 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday In 1976, German virologist Harald zur Hausen, MD, DSc, proposedopens in a new tab or window that human papillomavirus (HPV) causes cervical cancer, countering the widely held belief that herpes simplex virus caused the cancer. Seven years later, he proved the theory, showing that HPV genes are incorporated into cervical cancer cells. Subsequently, HPV has been shown to cause anal, penile, vulvar, vaginal, and oropharyngeal cancers, in addition to cervical cancer. Some patients with esophageal cancer test positive for HPV, which has been linked to other gastrointestinal cancers, notably anal cancer, but also gastric and colorectal cancer. The authors of a recent reviewopens in a new tab or window cited estimates of HPV positivity in esophageal adenocarcinoma (EAC), the predominant histologic subtype in the U.S., ranging from 13% to 35%. Estimates vary broadly by geography. For example, a systematic reviewopens in a new tab or window of 15 mostly small studies, published almost a decade ago, showed HPV positivity in up to 93% of EAC specimens. A U.S. study included in the review showed an HPV-positive rate of 16.8%. Much of the contemporary data on HPV and esophageal cancer come from Asia, which has a much higher incidence of esophageal cancer as compared with the U.S. The American Cancer Society estimates that 22,530 esophageal cancersopens in a new tab or window will be diagnosed in the U.S. in 2026, whereas the global incidenceopens in a new tab or window exceeds 500,000 cases annually. In all likelihood, the incidence of HPV-positive esophageal cancer in the U.S. is proportionally smaller. Precise current estimates are hard to come by, but the incidence is "much smaller than what we see in Asia," said Allan Lima Pereira, MD, PhD, of Moffitt Cancer Center in Tampa, Florida. "If HPV has any relationship in terms of causing esophageal cancers, this would be in the low single digits," he told MedPage Today. "It would be far below the 20% that is often cited from Asian studies." Pieces of a Puzzle As Pereira's comments suggest, a causal link between HPV and esophageal cancer remains controversial. Multiple studies have provided pieces of potential evidence. However, even when viewed from a cumulative perspective, the evidence falls short of definitive. One piece of evidence comes from the observation of papillomatous lesionsopens in a new tab or window in the esophagus, raising the possibility that a virus could be involved in the formation of esophageal cancer. In cattle, bovine papillomavirus (BPV) has been linked to papillomas and carcinomas in the upper gastrointestinal tract. Most BPV infections regress spontaneouslyopens in a new tab or window, but when cattle feed on a specific type of fern, papillomatosis persists and cancer eventually emerges. Studies have shown that BPV has transforming activityopens in a new tab or window in bovine cells. Another piece of evidence relates to the relationship between HPV and cancers that arise in transitional or transformational zones. Most cervical and oropharyngeal cancers, both caused primarily by HPV infection, arise in transitional zones, and transforming HPV infections associated with both types of cancer tend to concentrate in transitional zones. EAC also has a propensity to occur in the gastroesophageal junction, also a transition zone. A study published more than a decade agoopens in a new tab or window showed a significant association between high-risk HPV genotypes 16 and 18 and both Barrett's dysplasia (an esophageal cancer precursor) and EAC. In a separate reportopens in a new tab or window, the same researchers showed increasing viral load and integration of HPV 16 and 18 in association with increasing disease severity, from esophageal metaplasia to dysplasia to EAC. The researchers also found that HPV DNA was more likely to be located in the transformation zone. The dichotomous relationshipopens in a new tab or window between EAC and Barrett's esophagus provides yet another piece of indirect evidence. The historical model of esophageal cancer posits that the cancer arises from chronic gastroesophageal reflux and Barrett's esophagus. Since the 1970s, the incidence of EAC in Western nations has increased by 600%. However, that increase has been accompanied by a decline in the association of EAC with Barrett's esophagus. The association of HPV 16 and 18 with Barrett's esophagus and EAC offers one potential explanation for the anomaly. The rise of HPV-associated oropharyngeal canceropens in a new tab or window lends support to that viewpoint. Since the 1980s, HPV prevalence in oropharyngeal cancers has increased four- to fivefold. Evidence continues to accumulate. A recent meta-analysisopens in a new tab or window that included more than 13,000 patients with esophageal cancer showed significant associations for both HPV 16 and 18. The authors concluded that infection with either high-risk HPV strain "may be a strong co-factor along with other confounding risk factors for the etiopathogenesis of [esophageal cancer]." An even larger meta-analysis published earlier this yearopens in a new tab or window showed an overall HPV prevalence of 31% in 18,913 cases of esophageal cancer. However, the data showed that "HPV prevalence was consistently higher" in esophageal squamous cell cancer (ESCC) compared to EAC. "HPV infection, particularly HPV-16, is epidemiologically associated with esophageal cancer, with higher detection rates observed in ESCC," the authors concluded. "However, current evidence is insufficient to establish HPV as a definitive causal driver of [esophageal cancer], especially for EAC, due to the predominance of observational designs and limited mechanistic confirmation." The association with ESCC makes sense, said Pereira, noting that other types of HPV-related cancers are also squamous in origin, such as oropharyngeal cancer. "If you go deeper in the literature, you might find some data suggesting that HPV could be associated with some adenocarcinomas, but it would be very difficult to create a causation," he said. "The impression is that HPV would be more often a kind of a bystander than a driver in adenocarcinoma." Clinical Implications: Not There Yet With respect to clinical implications of HPV in esophageal cancer, the National Comprehensive Cancer Network (NCCN) does not include HPV in recommended biomarker testing. In fact, the NCCN guidelines for esophageal cancer make no mention of HPV. Guideline panelist Shadia Jalal, MD, of the Indiana University Simon Comprehensive Cancer Center in Indianapolis, told MedPage Today that "a lot more research is needed." In the same vein, the American Cancer Societyopens in a new tab or window acknowledges that HPV has been identified in esophageal cancers, especially in Asia and South Africa, but also noted that "HPV infection has not been shown to be a risk factor for esophageal cancer in other parts of the world, including the U.S." "My honest opinion is that the evidence [linking HPV and esophageal cancer] currently falls short of establishing HPV as a definitive cause of esophageal cancer," said Pereira. "But I think it cannot be dismissed entirely. I think it's not over." Issues related to geographic variation, different histologies, methods for detecting and quantifying HPV infection, and other factors confound the reproducibility required to draw definitive conclusions, he added. Time
Published on 2026-08-04; event discussed is historical and ongoing research debate with no single event date reported.
Location and population
Global context with focus on the United States and Asia; esophageal cancer incidence data referenced for U.S. (22,530 cases in 2026) and globally (>500,000 cases annually).
Event
A review article summarizing evidence on the association between human papillomavirus (HPV) and esophageal cancer, noting circumstantial evidence but lack of definitive proof of causation; reported by MedPage Today, Senior Editor Charles Bankhead.
Scale, impact and risk
In the U.S., HPV-positive esophageal cancer incidence is estimated to be in the 'low single digits' (below 20%), far lower than Asian estimates (up to 93% in some older studies); HPV prevalence in esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) is higher than in esophageal adenocarcinoma (EAC); global esophageal cancer cases exceed 500,000 annually. No explicit risk level stated.
Response, status and uncertainty
Evidence remains inconclusive regarding HPV as a definitive causal driver of esophageal cancer, particularly for EAC; NCCN guidelines do not recommend HPV biomarker testing for esophageal cancer; ongoing research and meta-analyses continue to explore associations, with calls for more mechanistic confirmation.

顶部 首页 首页 多云 / 24° 隐私 昆明造!首款覆盖HPV58型的三价疫苗获批上市 都市时报 8月6日,记者从康乐卫士(昆明)生物技术有限公司获悉,该公司收到国家药品监督管理局核准签发的三价HPV疫苗药品注册证书,标志着“滇中新区产”三价HPV疫苗正式获准上市。 此次获准上市的三价HPV疫苗是首款覆盖HPV58型的低价次疫苗,能够对亚非地区女性高危型别形成精准覆盖,是HPV疫苗研发领域取得的又一突破。 疫苗研发过程中,康乐卫士在覆盖HPV16/18型的基础上,创新性纳入HPV58型,可将女性宫颈癌的保护范围从70%提升至78%,填补了当前低价次HPV疫苗的精准防护空白。 连日来,位于滇中新区临空产业园的康乐卫士昆明生产基地疫苗生产车间里,生产设备运转,工作有序推进。 “三价HPV疫苗的获准上市是康乐卫士商业化征程的起点,实现了从研发驱动型生物科技企业向具备商业化生产与市场运营能力的综合制药企业的关键跨越。”该公司相关负责人表示,目前公司已搭建起国内布局最为丰富的HPV疫苗迭代矩阵,将加快推进后续九价、十五价HPV疫苗产品上市、市场准入与商业化推广工作。 来源:健康昆明 编辑:马东 审核:钟玲 Free web novels for online reading. 评论 全部 还没有人评论过,快来抢首评 抢首评 推荐阅读 艾美疫苗(06660.HK):8月7日南向资金减持7600股 证券之星港... 40分钟前 1 康希诺生物(06185.HK):8月7日南向资金减持15.62万股 证券之星港... 40分钟前 1 河北皮肤硬化面具脸风湿诊疗专家选择参考方向 平安医院免... 2小时前 237 Make Your Own Ocean Pavers Cheaply With This MoldTopchicdeals | SponsoredSponsored Shop Now Undo 想打造自然妈生鼻可以参考哪些技术方向 北京隆鼻医... 2小时前 170 8.8日:阿尔瓦雷斯向西蒙尼递交离队申请!马竞5500万转会费恐成筹码 黎夕爱情 2小时前 749 怎么在昆明选到适配需求的旧房全改及二手房翻新服务 壹山间设计 2小时前 18 2026盘点:昆明无缝管生产厂家哪家好?云南龙铁商贸,为工程项目选对供货商 云南龙铁钢... 2小时前 22 两性关系:70岁夫妻悟透“舍”字,不争不改不盼,往后越活越舒心 暧心情感 3小时前 27 晚餐才是胖瘦的分水线!坚持吃对两样东西,醒来体重直降! 心灵书舫 2026.7.16 1.5万 河北难治型再障诊疗可选择的经验丰富从业者有哪些 平安医院血... 3小时前 236 8.8日:面对全球,用中国话骂中国,高市这次,倒是撞到了朝鲜枪口上 堇墨爱情 4小时前 373 谁在穿越7月北京楼市淡季?改善需求撑起“慢修复”行情 新京报 4小时前 3372 八月八日:不准备打俄罗斯了?乌元首转向中国,对我们外交任务下达,中乌局势又变? B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

性与癌症

Sex and cancer
新闻简报The article 'Sex and Cancer' published on August 4, 2026, discusses the role of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) in causing cervical cancer in women and various cancers in men. It highlights that HPV is responsible for the majority of cervical cancer cases and is also linked to throat cancer, with the US CDC reporting approximately 260,000 to 270,000 HPV-associated cancers diagnosed in women and 210,000 to 230,000 in men over the past decade. The article notes that in the Philippines, about 1.15 to 1.5 million women are infected with HPV, and 38 million to 39.6 million are at risk for complications. It emphasizes that Gardasil vaccines can prevent HPV-associated cancers when given before sexual activity, but the rate of throat cancer in the US has remained stagnant, possibly due to HPV transmission through oral sex. The article also mentions that between half a million and a million Americans have genital warts, and 13 million new cases of cervical cancer are discovered annually. The article calls for increased vaccination and early diagnosis to combat these cancers, but does not provide specific details on current prevention efforts or their effectiveness.
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-08-04。它讨论了过去十年与 HPV 相关的癌症统计数据,并提到了过去二十年的趋势分析。
地点与对象
本文重点关注美国的菲律宾裔美国人社区,特别是亚利桑那州、夏威夷州、中西部、德克萨斯州西北部、加利福尼亚州、洛杉矶县、圣地亚哥县和北加州内华达州拉斯维加斯。它还提到了菲律宾,特别是马尼拉大都会区和该国的总人口。
事件
文章报道,人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 导致女性大部分 宫颈癌,并导致男性各种癌症。它还提到 HPV 与咽喉癌有关,而 Gardasil 疫苗 可以预防这些癌症。
规模、影响与风险
Approximately 42 million Americans are infected with HPV, with about 13 million new infections yearly. In the Philippines, about 1.15 to 1.5 million women carry an active HPV infection, and 38 million to 39.6 million are at risk for complications. Over the past decade, an estimated 260,000 to 270,000 HPV-associated cancers were diagnosed in women, and 210,000 to 230,000 among men in the US. The article also states that between half a million and a million Americans have genital warts, and 13 million new cases of cervical cancer are discovered annually.
应对、状态与不确定性
文章提到,在性活动前服用 Gardasil 疫苗 可以预防 HPV 相关癌症。报告还指出,美国的喉癌发病率并未下降,某些人群的口咽癌发病率有所增加。文章强调了 疫苗接种 和早期 诊断 对于预防 宫颈癌 的重要性,但没有提供有关当前预防工作或其有效性的具体细节。

It highlights that HPV is responsible for the majority of cervical cancer cases and is also linked to throat cancer, with the US CDC reporting approximately 260,000 to 270,000 HPV-associated cancers diagnosed in women and 210,000 to 230,000 in men over the past decade. The article notes that in the Philippines, about 1.15 to 1.5 million women are infected with HPV, and 38 million to 39.6 million are at risk for complications. It emphasizes that Gardasil vaccines can prevent HPV-associated cancers when given before sexual activity, but the rate of throat cancer in the US has remained stagnant, possibly due to HPV transmission through oral sex. The article also mentions that between half a million and a million Americans have genital warts, and 13 million new cases of cervical cancer are discovered annually. The article calls for increased vaccination and early diagnosis to combat these cancers, but does not provide specific details on current prevention efforts or their effectiveness.

ChuaAugust 4, 2026013 mins Share Post Viber Email While Human Papillomavirus (HPV), a sexually transmitted virus, causes the majority of cervical cancer in women, it is also responsible for various cancers in men. Today, approximately 42 million Americans are infected with HPV, with about 13 million new infections yearly. In the Philippines, about 1.15 to 1.5 million Filipino women carry an active HPV at any given time, and about 38 million to 39.6 million are considered at risk for developing complications. Over the past decades, an estimated “260,000 to 270,000 HPV-associated cancers were diagnosed in women, and 210,000 to 230,000 among men, in the United States over the past decade,” according to the US CDC. These are all preventable with the Gardasil vaccines, when boys and girls are vaccinated before they are sexually active. The rate of throat cancer in the United States has not declined, compared to most head and neck malignancies. I suspect that the statistics could also be true, when extrapolated, for other countries, like the Philippines, since most Filipinos are westernized in their lifestyle, habits, and behaviors, etc. The most logical explanation why cancer of the throat has not diminished has been attributed to the Human Papillomavirus. HPV is popularly known to cause genital and most cancers of the cervix (mouth of the womb). It has only been recently that scientists discovered and identified HPV transmission through oral sex as an etiology of throat cancer. M.D. Anderson Cancer Center in Houston reported that its research team found “the incidence of throat cancer (in the United States) to be stagnant and even rising in some populations, defying a downward trend in other head and neck cancers linked more closely with smoking.” The American Cancer Society reports that the greatest risk factors in head and neck cancers are smoking and drinking alcoholic beverages, 90% of them either smokers or tobacco-chewers and about 80% of them also imbibed a lot of alcohol. The good news is that a trend analysis in head and neck cancers in the United States shows a decline over the past twenty years, trailing a decrease in smoking prevalence, which started in the 1970s, by 10 to 15 years. The bad news is that oropharyngeal cancers (which include the tonsils, base of the tongue and soft palate, and side and back of the throat) have been up in some populations in the United States, and probably among people in other parts of the world who practice oral sex, where HPV takes its toll. HPV could also cause cancer of the vagina, penis, and anus, besides cervical and throat cancers. The sample of the vaccine referred to is Gardasil, which is genetically engineered and which blocks infection caused by two of the more than 100 types of Human Papillomavirus (HPV), strains 16 and 18. These two sexually-transmitted viruses are responsible for about 70% of cervical cancers. HPV, in one form or the other, afflicts more than 20 million Americans. The other strains of the virus cause painful genital warts, and sometimes, cervical cancers too. Let us momentarily discuss cervical cancer, since the HP virus is implicated in its causation in almost three-quarters of this malignancy. How prevalent is cervical cancer? In the Philippines, there are about 5000 new cases of cervical cancer each year. However, more alarming than that is the fact that there are between 10,000 to 25,000 women walking around (not seen by a physician) who have undiagnosed pre-invasive lesions in their cervix. If diagnosed early, these women could be saved. For every four survivors of breast cancer, there are fewer than 3 women who survive cervical cancer, which shows how virulent cervical cancer is. What causes cervical cancer? Human Papillomavirus (HPV, also known as genital herpes virus) accounts for most cervical cancers. At least 50% of sexually active men and women are infected with genital HPV, especially those with multiple partners. Among the million American and Philippine men and women infected with HPV, many linked with abnormal Papare tests, genital warts, and cervical cancer. It is estimated that at least 13 million new cases of cervical cancers are discovered annually. Between half a million and a million Americans have genital warts, transmitted through sexual contact. It takes time for those infected with HPV to develop into cancer, so there are countless infected people out there who would potentially have cancers. The best for children (before turning 10) and adults up to age 45 is to get vaccinated to prevent HPV infection to begin with. Is the cure for cervical cancer? Better than the cure! A vaccine (Gardasil) that prevents cervical cancer, vulvar and vaginal cancer that was approved by the US-FDA in 2006 was found to be “effective 100%, in the short term, at blocking the cancer and lesions likely to turn to cancer” (like the pre-invasive lesions), according to Gardasil manufacturer, Merck. To have 100 percent efficacy is something we see very rarely. The UK’s version of the vaccine is known as Cervarix. How early should the vaccine be given? Students in grammar school, middle school and high school should be vaccinated before they become sexually active, as early as 9 years old for both boys and girls, and adults up to age 45, because once they catch HPV infection, there is no cure; herpes is for life. This was the recommendation of Dr. Gloria Bachmann, director of The Women’s Health Institute at Robert Wood Johnson Medical School in Brunswick, NJ, who said this vaccine is a “phenomenal breakthrough.” Prophylaxis Gardasil vaccination comprehensively eliminates HPV 16 and 18-associated non-invasive and invasive cervical cancer. The vaccine also cuts down infection with HPV 6 and 11, the causes of 90% of genital warts. Gardasil is safe for both boys and girls and adults. How about throat cancers? Of the 45,000 head and neck cancers in the US each year, about 10,000 of them are oropharyngeal cancers, and tongue cancers among young adults have also increased. The evident conclusion is that the cause is the HP virus. “Over the last five years, 35% of the throat cancer patients treated at the M.D. Anderson Cancer Center had no history of smoking, and that close to 90% of patients who had never smoked showed evidence of oral infection with HPV. The researchers felt that “vaccinating only females against HPV could result in a missed opportunity to prevent throat cancers.” It is recommended for both girls and boys to close that gap, preventing boys from spreading the HPV virus. The use of cling plastic wrap used in the kitchen placed over the woman’s groin for oral sex as a shield has been advocated by some to prevent HPV transmission. Mouth, tongue, and other throat cancers could be as grave and deadly as most other forms of cancer. I do not know how to put this more seriously, more effectively, and more delicately, but medically speaking, a moratorium on oral sex might be in order. Realistically, human nature usually prevails. This is a greater argument for childhood vaccination with the life-saving Gardasil. And parents should not deny their sons and daughters this valuable vaccine. * * * The opinions, beliefs and viewpoints expressed by the author do not necessarily reflect the opinions, beliefs and viewpoints of the Asian Journal, its management, editorial board and staff. * * * Philip S. 单剂 HPV 疫苗 可能提供与两剂相同的保护以预防 宫颈癌:研究

Single dose of HPV vaccine may offer same protection as two doses to prevent cervical cancer: Study
新闻简报发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》上的一项由 NIH 资助的大型研究表明,单剂 HPV 疫苗 可以提供与标准两剂方案相同的针对持续性宫颈 HPV 感染的保护作用。这项在哥斯达黎加对 20,000 多名 12-16 岁女孩进行的随机试验发现,在所有给药方案中,针对 HPV 16 和 18 型的有效性至少为 97%。HPV 会导致 宫颈癌 和其他恶性肿瘤,但全球青少年女孩中 疫苗接种 的覆盖率仍低于 30%。如果得到证实,这些发现可以简化给药并扩大可及性,特别是在资源匮乏的环境中,完成两剂疫苗具有挑战性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Single dose of HPV vaccine may offer same protection as two doses to prevent cervical cancer: Study

News Brief
A large NIH-funded study published in the New England Journal of Medicine suggests that a single dose of HPV vaccine may provide the same protection against persistent cervical HPV infections as the standard two-dose regimen. The randomized trial, conducted in Costa Rica with more than 20,000 girls aged 12–16, found at least 97% effectiveness against HPV types 16 and 18 across all dosing schedules. HPV causes cervical cancer and other malignancies, yet global vaccination coverage among adolescent girls remains below 30%. If confirmed, the findings could simplify delivery and expand access, particularly in low-resource settings where two-dose completion is challenging.
Original source text (English)
3 Min Read A single dose of the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine, which protects against a virus that can cause cervical cancer and several other cancers, may work just as well as the standard two-dose schedule, according to a new study. The study, funded by the US National Institutes of Health (NIH) and published in the New England Journal of Medicine, was led by Dr Aimee Kreimer of the National Cancer Institute (NCI), part of NIH, and Dr Carolina Porras of the Agencia Costarricense de Investigaciones Biomedicas in Costa Rica. HPV is a common virus that spreads through sexual contact. While most HPV infections go away on their own, some types of the virus can lead to cervical cancer. HPV can also cause cancers of the throat, anus, penis, vagina and vulva, as well as genital warts. For years, doctors have recommended two doses of the HPV vaccine for adolescents. But the new study found that one dose provides the same level of protection. The study involved more than 20,000 girls aged 12 to 16 in Costa Rica, who were enrolled between November 2017 and February 2020. Researchers also surveyed more than 3,000 unvaccinated women and girls to estimate HPV infection levels among those who had not received the vaccine. Participants were randomly divided into groups. They received either one dose of a bivalent HPV vaccine or a nonavalent HPV vaccine. Six months after the first vaccination, half of the participants received a second dose of the same HPV vaccine, while the other half received a vaccine against tetanus, diphtheria and pertussis as a comparison group. The researchers found that one dose of the HPV vaccine protected against persistent cervical HPV infections that was no worse than protection provided by two doses. The study found that all vaccine schedules were at least 97% effective in preventing persistent infections caused by HPV types 16 and 18. "The findings suggest that a single dose of either HPV vaccine will provide adequate protection against HPV infection and subsequent cervical cancers as effectively as two doses," said Kreimer. The findings could make it easier for millions of girls worldwide to get vaccinated, especially in countries where access to vaccines remains limited. The study, funded by the US National Institutes of Health (NIH) and published in the New England Journal of Medicine, was led by Dr Aimee Kreimer of the National Cancer Institute (NCI), part of NIH, and Dr Carolina Porras of the Agencia Costarricense de Investigaciones Biomedicas in Costa Rica. HPV is a common virus that spreads through sexual contact. While most HPV infections go away on their own, some types of the virus can lead to cervical cancer. HPV can also cause cancers of the throat, anus, penis, vagina and vulva, as well as genital warts. While many HPV infections clear on their own, certain cancer-causing types of the virus can lead to cervical cancer, which kills around 2.5 lakh women worldwide every year. Vaccine coverage remains low globally, with less than 30% of adolescent girls worldwide receiving the vaccine. For years, doctors have recommended two doses of the HPV vaccine for adolescents. But the new study found that one dose provides the same level of protection. The study involved more than 20,000 girls aged 12 to 16 in Costa Rica, who were enrolled between November 2017 and February 2020. Researchers also surveyed more than 3,000 unvaccinated women and girls to estimate HPV infection levels among those who had not received the vaccine. Participants were randomly divided into groups. They received either one dose of a bivalent HPV vaccine or a nonavalent HPV vaccine. The bivalent vaccine protects against HPV types 16 and 18, which cause more than 77% of cervical cancer cases worldwide. The nonavalent vaccine protects against these two cancer-causing HPV types, along with five additional cancer-related HPV types and two types that cause genital warts. Six months after the first vaccination, half of the participants received a second dose of the same HPV vaccine, while the other half received a vaccine against tetanus, diphtheria and pertussis as a comparison group. The researchers found that one dose of the HPV vaccine protected against persistent cervical HPV infections that was no worse than protection provided by two doses. The study found that all vaccine schedules were at least 97% effective in preventing persistent infections caused by HPV types 16 and 18. "The findings suggest that a single dose of either HPV vaccine will provide adequate protection against HPV infection and subsequent cervical cancers as effectively as two doses," said Kreimer. The findings could make it easier for millions of girls worldwide to get vaccinated, especially in countries where access to vaccines remains limited. (Edited by : Sudarsanan Mani)
