抗癌口香糖可将 HPV 水平降低高达 93%

Cancer-fighting chewing gum cuts HPV levels by up to 93%
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚大学的研究人员报告称,生物工程口香糖使头颈癌患者唾液中的 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 水平大幅降低了 93%,口腔冲洗液样本中的 HPV 水平降低了 80%,同时几乎消除了与这些癌症相关的两种细菌。这种口香糖采用 抗病毒药物 蛋白和抗菌肽设计,保留了有益的口腔细菌,并被提议作为一种潜在的辅助治疗或预防措施。《科学报告》中发表的研究结果是对患者样本进行离体测试后得出的结果,强调需要采用新方法来治疗头颈鳞状细胞癌,因为头颈鳞状细胞癌在晚期阶段的结果仍然很差。该团队呼吁将该疗法推进临床试验。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件日期:提供的证据中未报告。发布日期:2026 年 8 月 4 日(N4)。
地点与对象
美国宾夕法尼亚大学牙科医学院(N9、N30)。
事件
据报道,一种含有 抗病毒药物 和抗菌剂的生物工程口香糖可降低头颈鳞状细胞癌 (HNSCC) 患者口腔样本中的 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 水平,同时减少与 HNSCC 相关的两种细菌(牙龈卟啉单胞菌和具核梭杆菌)。研究结果发表在科学报告中,并作为潜在的辅助治疗或预防措施提出(N16、N18、N20-N23、N26、N27、N40、N41)。
规模、影响与风险
唾液样本中的 HPV 水平降低了 93%,口腔冲洗液样本中的 HPV 水平降低了 80%;单次给药后,牙龈卟啉单胞菌和具核梭杆菌水平降至几乎为零。治疗 保留了有益的口腔细菌。证据中没有明确说明风险级别 (N20-N24)。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用了 HNSCC 患者的离体临床样本。该口香糖采用 FRIL(抗病毒药物 蛋白)和 protegrin(抗菌肽)进行工程设计。作者建议进入临床试验作为辅助治疗或预防。资金来源包括 NIH、加州大学洛杉矶分校学术参议院和 NCI 癌症中心支持拨款。没有报告人体试验、监管批准或临床实施细节(N17、N22、N26、N27、N29、N36、N41)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Cancer-fighting chewing gum cuts HPV levels by up to 93%

News Brief
Researchers at the University of Pennsylvania report that a bioengineered chewing gum sharply reduced human papillomavirus (HPV) levels by up to 93% in saliva and 80% in oral rinse samples from head and neck cancer patients, alongside near-elimination of two bacteria linked to these cancers. The gum, engineered with an antiviral protein and an antimicrobial peptide, preserved beneficial oral bacteria and is proposed as a potential adjunct therapy or preventive measure. Findings published in Scientific Reports follow ex vivo testing on patient samples and highlight the need for new approaches to head and neck squamous cell carcinoma, where outcomes remain poor at later stages. The team calls for advancing the therapy to clinical trials.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Your source for the latest research news Follow: Facebook X/Twitter Subscribe: RSS Feeds Newsletter New! Sign up for our free email newsletter. Science News from research organizations Cancer-fighting chewing gum cuts HPV levels by up to 93% Bioengineered chewing gum may offer a surprisingly powerful new way to fight microbes linked to head and neck cancer. Date: August 4, 2026 Source: University of Pennsylvania Summary: A specially engineered chewing gum reduced HPV by up to 93% and nearly eliminated two bacteria linked to head and neck cancer. The treatment preserved beneficial mouth bacteria, raising hopes for a safer and more affordable therapy. Share: Facebook Twitter Pinterest LinkedIN Email FULL STORY Researchers developed a bioengineered chewing gum that sharply reduced three microbes linked to head and neck cancers. Credit: Shutterstock Researchers have developed a bioengineered chewing gum that may offer a new way to target microbes associated with head and neck cancer. In tests involving oral samples from patients, extracts from the gum sharply reduced one virus and two types of bacteria linked to the disease. The research team was led by Henry Daniell of the School of Dental Medicine at the University of Pennsylvania. The findings were published in Scientific Reports and could support the development of more accessible and affordable treatments. A Need for Better Head and Neck Cancer Therapies Head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) is a common form of cancer that begins in the tissues lining the mouth and throat. The disease can be particularly aggressive, and outcomes are often poor when it is discovered at a later stage. Daniell says that many recently approved cancer medications have not produced major improvements in patients' quality of life or five-year survival. That limited progress highlights the need for new approaches that can work alongside existing treatments. Targeting HPV and Harmful Oral Bacteria The new study builds on earlier research involving chewing gum made from lablab beans (bean gum). The gum contains FRIL, a naturally occurring antiviral protein. Daniell and his colleagues used oral samples from patients with HNSCC to study three microbes associated with cancer. These included human papillomavirus, or HPV, along with two bacterial species, Porphyromonas gingivalis (Pg) and Fusobacterium nucleatum (Fn). "The global increase in oropharyngeal cancer is linked to HPV infection," says Daniell. "And Pg and Fn infections worsen survival rates of untreated recurrent or metastatic oral cancer, even after surgery and risk-adjusted adjuvant, or supplemental, therapies." Gum Extracts Sharply Reduce Microbe Levels Tests showed that extracts from the bean gum lowered HPV levels by 93% in saliva samples. HPV levels also fell by 80% in oral rinse samples. The researchers then engineered the bean gum to contain protegrin, an antimicrobial peptide capable of killing harmful bacteria. A single dose brought levels of Pg and Fn down to almost zero. Importantly, the treatment did not appear to harm the beneficial bacteria that normally live in the mouth. Radiation therapy can have a different effect, reducing helpful bacteria while encouraging the growth of disease-causing yeast (Candida albicans). A Potential Addition to Cancer Treatment The ability to target dangerous microbes while preserving the healthy oral microbiome could make the gum useful in several ways. Researchers believe it may eventually serve as an additional therapy alongside current cancer treatments or as a preventive measure against infection and transmission. "Lip and oral cavity cancer was the seventh leading cancer type in cancer incidence and mortality rate worldwide in adolescents, young adults, and middle-aged adults in 2022," says Daniell. "Our findings support the value of advancing these therapies to clinical trials as adjuvants with current treatments or as prophylaxis to prevent infection and transmission." Henry Daniell is the W.D. Miller Professor in the Department of Basic & Translational Sciences at the School of Dental Medicine at the University of Pennsylvania. Other authors include Geetanjali Wakade, Rahul Singh, and Smruti Nair of Penn Dental Medicine; Andrés M. Bur and Sufi M. Thomas of the University of Kansas Medical Center; Eri S. Srivatsan and Marilene B. Wang of the University of California at Los Angeles; and Saroj K. Basak of the Veterans Administration Greater Los Angeles Healthcare System. The research received support from NIH (grant 5-R01-HL 107904-13 awarded to Henry Daniell), the Academic Senate Grant of the David Geffen School of Medicine at UCLA (the Surgical Education Research program), and the National Cancer Institute Cancer Center (Support Grant P30 CA168524). View five news elements
Time
Event date: Not reported in the supplied evidence. Publication date: 4 August 2026 (N4).
Location and population
University of Pennsylvania School of Dental Medicine, United States (N9, N30).
Event
A bioengineered chewing gum engineered to contain antiviral and antibacterial agents was reported to reduce human papillomavirus (HPV) levels in oral samples from patients with head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC), alongside reductions in two bacteria linked to HNSCC (Porphyromonas gingivalis and Fusobacterium nucleatum). The findings were published in Scientific Reports and presented as a potential adjunct therapy or preventive measure (N16, N18, N20-N23, N26, N27, N40, N41).
Scale, impact and risk
HPV levels reduced by 93% in saliva samples and 80% in oral rinse samples; Porphyromonas gingivalis and Fusobacterium nucleatum levels reduced to nearly zero after a single dose. The treatment preserved beneficial oral bacteria. No explicit risk level was stated in the evidence (N20-N24).
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used ex vivo clinical samples from HNSCC patients. The gum was engineered with FRIL (antiviral protein) and protegrin (antimicrobial peptide). Authors propose advancing to clinical trials as an adjuvant therapy or prophylaxis. Funding sources include NIH, UCLA Academic Senate, and NCI Cancer Center Support Grant. No human trials, regulatory approvals, or clinical implementation details were reported (N17, N22, N26, N27, N29, N36, N41).

Original source text (English)
Home Radio TV Videos Movies Advertise Ghana Contact Login NEWS SPORTS BUSINESS POLITICS OPINIONS HEALTH AFRICA WORLD ENTERTAINMENT LIFESTYLE SCIENCE TECHNOLOGY NOLLYWOOD MEDIA MEMBERS Archive Health News Family/Parenting Home/Food Health/Fitness Spirituality OnAIR Search CDA Consult calls for greater focus on cervical cancer prevention, urges support for free HPV vaccination By Kristodia Otibu Asiedu II Contributor Health MON, 03 AUG 2026 Executive Director of CDA Consult, Mr. Francis Ameyibor The Communication for Development and Advocacy Consult (CDA Consult) has called for intensified national efforts to combat cervical cancer, urging stakeholders to give the disease the same level of attention and public advocacy as breast cancer. The organisation stressed that tackling women's cancers requires a comprehensive approach that combines sustained breast cancer awareness with stronger education on cervical cancer prevention. Speaking during a media engagement in Koforidua in the Eastern Region over the weekend, the Executive Director of CDA Consult, Mr. Francis Ameyibor, said promoting awareness of both diseases would strengthen prevention efforts and create “a shield for generations,” by equipping women and girls with the knowledge needed to safeguard their long-term health. He noted that although breast cancer receives significant public attention during annual awareness campaigns, cervical cancer—despite being largely preventable through regular screening and Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination—continues to receive limited public education. According to him, the lack of awareness, stigma associated with gynaecological examinations and limited access to routine screening in some communities have contributed to late diagnosis and avoidable deaths among women. Mr. Ameyibor urged the media to play a leading role in promoting cervical cancer awareness, noting that many women delay seeking medical attention until the disease has reached an advanced stage. “Many women only seek medical attention when symptoms have advanced, reducing the chances of successful treatment,” he said. He called on media organisations, civil society groups, faith-based organisations and community health stakeholders to join a coordinated advocacy campaign promoting regular medical check-ups, early screening and HPV vaccination. Mr. Ameyibor reaffirmed CDA Consult's commitment to mobilising health professionals, traditional and religious leaders, government institutions and civil society organisations to intensify efforts aimed at eliminating cervical cancer in Ghana. He said integrating cervical cancer education into mainstream public health campaigns would provide women with accurate information and encourage early detection and treatment. The Executive Director also appealed to parents and guardians to ensure that eligible girls receive the HPV vaccine, describing it as one of the most effective interventions for preventing cervical cancer later in life. He said CDA Consult would continue to promote cervical cancer awareness through community outreach programmes, media partnerships, workplace education and digital advocacy campaigns. Mr. Ameyibor welcomed the government's decision to introduce the HPV vaccine into Ghana's routine immunisation programme for girls aged nine years and acknowledged the support of Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, UNICEF, the World Health Organization (WHO) and other development partners. He noted that the nationwide HPV vaccination campaign, scheduled to begin in September, targets girls between the ages of nine and 14 and represents a major milestone in Ghana's efforts to eliminate cervical cancer by 2030. According to him, CDA Consult has since 2023 been implementing a nationwide Change Paradigm Advocacy Campaign for Free HPV Vaccination to promote prevention, early detection and treatment of cervical cancer. He explained that the organisation regularly brings together health professionals, human rights advocates, gender activists, religious leaders and communication practitioners to educate the public on cervical cancer prevention and broader health issues. The advocacy campaign, he said, combines calls for free HPV vaccination with sustained public education on screening, diagnosis and treatment. Mr. Ameyibor noted that the campaign is being carried out under the national theme, "Ghana Must Rise Against Cervical Cancer," with regional-specific slogans and advocacy strategies designed to strengthen public education across the country. “Our team will work with regional health professionals to embark on vigorous education across the districts to ensure that the message to combat cervical cancer through vaccination, screening, diagnosis and treatment is spread across,” he said. He described the inclusion of the HPV vaccine in Ghana's national immunisation schedule as a major breakthrough in the country's fight against cervical cancer. “As holistic health advocates, CDA Consult is pleased for the nationwide initiative and more delighted for the government has adopted the HPV vaccination into the national routine immunization schedules for girls; this is a major breakthrough,” Mr. Ameyibor noted.
